Betuma is a Syriac Compound, whofe true Orthography is Sel'-Touma, which both in Arabic and Syriac, is tlie Houfe or Church of St. ‘Thomas. In like
manner the Syrians call the City of Martyropolis, Bargamea, or Beit-Garmea, and fo of others. But as much
as our Author leaves us in the dark as to the Courfe
he fleered; if we allow that Kaukam, or Conkan, as
the Portugucfe write it, is fomewhere about the Gulf
of Cambaya, and if we grant that their Navigation
was no better than Goading, which cannot well be
called in QuelUon j than need we not admire that the
Arabs fhould reckon it a Month’s Voyage from thence
to Betuma. Calabar, Sene/, Kadrange, or Chitran,
are in the Neighbourhood of St. ‘Twmash. Marco Polo
and almoll all tlie old Voyage Writers agree, that by
the Tradition of the Country St. ‘Thomas Ihould be interred in this very Place; and this is confirmed by
John di Empoli, Barbofa, Corfali, and almoll all the P . 146.
other lirll Travellers. This Tradition is ilill in force 312. 315I'
among the Nejloriam j and one of their moll famous
Authors, having fummarily touched upon the Preaching
of St. Thomas, lays, his Tomb was difcoveted upon Amr.Hlft,
the Sea Shore, in a Village famous for being the Scene Ar.M SS.
of hie Maitj'rdom. To this Tradition may be added of the
that of the Malabar, and molt of the other Syrian Jloriant,^
Churches, who all believe St. Thomas penetrated into the Indies, and that he there lies intombed: But
this lhall be treated more amply in the DilTertations
cn the the Ne/orian Churches.
